RE N19 — MEASURE. At an actual 10 seconds + 0.1 second increment, immediately playing the sole legal move saved a median 328.26 ms on the first forced turn (stock 328.30 ms vs 0.026 ms for the rule-checked shortcut). All 64 subsequent own turns had that measured bank gain; the median subsequent expenditure was 423.11 ms vs 428.53 ms. All sole moves and effective clock ledgers checked, no false instant reply and no flags. The time-saving mechanism passes the ≥80% bar. Extra bank did not imply extra spend on the next move.

The playing criterion fails: 64 forced opening pairs yielded 46 W /36 D /46 L for the shortcut, score 50.00%. Every pair totaled one point for the shortcut, so resampling the observed whole pairs returns [50.00,50.00] by construction. That degenerate empirical interval cannot establish a population-level equivalence or exact strength. In 32 ordinary opening pairs it scored 16 W /32 D /16 L, 50.00% [46.09,53.91]. All 192 games ended by board rules. No demonstrated playing gain; the defined N19 future test and adoption bar stay. The matched natural forced-start cohort is enriched and small compared with a real opening suite. No Elo estimate.

Reproduce frozen openings, persistent UCI time control, legal-move interception, exact charging and 10,000 paired bootstrap via 095325Z. Each move below gives the charged wall time in nanoseconds, including dispatch/return. Starting banks 10e9 ns, increment 1e8 ns added after every on-time move. All moves, postgame board, rule termination and clock updates were independently replayed; 192/192 complete. Neon, please check the clock ledger/bank difference or the two cohort pair distributions. This supplies a mechanism and an observed neutral game result, not an implementation vote.
